金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 05 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國製造業維持擴張，成本壓力升高：9 月 ISM 製造業調查顯示，美國製造業連續第九個月
位於擴張區間，新訂單與就業分項均見改善，反映接單與用人需求仍有支撐。然而，價格指數
由 8 月的 71.1 升至 9 月的 77.9，能源、金屬及關稅相關成本上升，加上供應商交貨偏慢，企
業面臨更大成本壓力 。 後續須觀察企業能否轉嫁成本 ， 以及通膨黏性對聯準會政策判斷的影響 。
◼ 日本短觀顯示製造業改善、服務業略緩：日本銀行 10 月 1 日公布的 9 月短觀顯示，大型製造

業景氣判斷指數由 6 月的＋22 升至＋24，大型非製造業則由＋37 降至＋35。企業預估 12 月
兩項指數分別回落至＋21 與＋30，顯示現況仍有支撐，後續看法則偏審慎。製造業改善有助
支撐日股獲利預期，但服務業放緩及日銀政策路徑仍須持續觀察。
◼ 台灣製造業活動加速擴張，AI 需求續強：10 月 1 日公布的製造業調查顯示，台灣 9 月製造業
活動擴張速度創 2021 年 8 月以來最快，AI 相關應用需求仍是重要推力。國發會 9 月 29 日公
布的 8 月景氣對策信號續呈紅燈，綜合判斷分數為 41 分，與 7 月持平，領先及同時指標皆續
升。科技供應鏈景氣仍有支撐，但成長動能能否擴及其他產業，仍是觀察後續景氣的重點。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，關注財報季表現： AI 基礎建設需求未受利率環境影響，而是持續擴張，帶
動光通訊、CCL、ABF 載板 、記憶體、先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持強勁成長，市場表
現由全面上漲轉向結構性分化。隨新一代 AI 伺服器放量、CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優

勢、定價能力及訂單能見度延伸至 2027 年的龍頭廠商仍可望受惠。10 月中旬起陸續公布的
第三季財報，若結果持續表現亮眼，可望為市場再添動能，屆時具 AI 供應鏈題材的台股與日
股亦可望受惠。
◼ 債券佈局聚焦 5~10 年期美國公債及投等債：雖然貨幣政策風險 下降有助於穩定長期通膨預
期，但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應，以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長
天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，5~10 年期債券較能兼顧票息收益與存續
期間風險管理。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公司債指數利差約 80-90 個基點，雖然利差
偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供具吸引力的絕對收益水準，因此現階段5~10 年期美國公債及
投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI 從 8 月的 55.4 降至 54.9，顯示服務業活動增速放
緩，然而國內需求強勁使供應鏈承壓，並推動投入價格指數由 72.6 升至 74.0 為
四年多來新高；另一方面，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 9 月的 48.1
降至 46.3，為連續第三個月下降且創 5 月以來最低 ， 惟未來一年通膨預期由 4.6%
升至 4.7%，顯示通膨可能持續維持高位至 2027 年。
⚫ 聯準會 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示與會官員均支持升息，並認為年底前尚需升息
一次 ，但對於後續升息時點及升息理由上存在分歧； 其中部分官員認為升息是防
止能源及其他價格衝擊造成更廣泛影響 的預防性措施，但更為鷹派官員則認為
升息在於抑制投資和支出，以防範正在顯現的需求驅動型通膨。
⚫ 歐洲央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示全體決策委員均同意升息，並認為當前經
濟成長風險偏向下行 ， 通膨風險則偏向上行 ，因此將保留政策彈性， 根據經濟數

據做為決策，不提供未來利率路徑指引。
⚫ 台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨警
戒線 ， 且創兩年半來最大漲幅 ，除了豪雨導致蔬果價格上漲及中秋假期的短期因
素外，油價居高不下亦使通膨壓力增加。
⚫ 中國央行發布匯率政策立場聲明， 強調中國貿易發展根源於產業國際競爭力的
提升，無必要通過匯率貶值獲取貿易競爭優勢 ，並指出中國長期實施以市場供需
為基礎、參考一籃子貨幣進行調節管理浮動匯率制度。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.699，低點在 31.940，以 31.875 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 4.5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，受到前週公布的美國非農就業數據低於預期，降低
市場對聯準會 10 月升息的預期， 台股創高吸引外資匯入 ， 推升台幣最高來到31.699；
不過隨著美伊緊張情勢升溫且台灣連假將至 ， 台股收低使外資轉為大幅賣超並匯出 ，
推低台幣回到 9 月中以來的震盪區間下緣，最低觸及 31.940，最終在央行調節之下
收在 31.875。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心
指數持續下滑，由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期
升至 4.7%，加上中東局勢反覆，油價高位震盪，仍支撐市場對於聯準會年內升息
的預期，預計美元指數維持在 102 附近震盪，對台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台灣 9 月出口創歷史新高，年增 60.9%，反映 AI 及科技需求持續強勁，出口商後
續拋匯需求可望提供台幣支撐；然而，台股逼近五萬點關卡之際，外資未能延續買
超走勢，且連假前已大幅賣超台股，若後續外資持續匯出，將限制台幣升值空間。
綜合以上，預估本週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪 。 中國十一長假期間 (10/1~10/7) 境內人民幣市場休市 ， 而
境外人民幣維持偏強格局 ， 主要在6.7000 上方震盪 ， 臨近周末 ， 人行中間價為6.7330，
創 3 年多來新高，加上美元指數漲勢放緩，推升境外人民幣突破 6.7000 整數關卡，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.6927，週線升值 134 點，境內人民幣收在 6.6929，較十一長
假前夕升值 121 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間偏升。就國際市場來看，美國密西根大學消費者信心指數
由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創5 月以來新低 ， 不過對於未來一年的通膨預期升至4.7%，
加上油價維持高檔 ， 市場對於聯準會年內升息的預期仍存 ， 美元指數維持高檔震盪 ，
將限制人民幣升值速度；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價維持偏強設定，不過人
行表示將防範短期大幅波動與急貶風險 ， 並無意透過人民幣貶值取得貿易競爭優勢 ，
有助於穩定市場對人民幣的預期 ；最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 各天期
均線仍向下發散，空頭格局不變，下方支撐先看向 9 月底低點 6.6908。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7100

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪走升。週初，因美元指數在102 附近盤整，帶領USD/JPY 延
續前一週走勢震盪走升到 158 上方；週間，因缺乏重要經濟數據，市場偏向觀望，
USD/JPY 維持在 158 附近震盪；臨近週末，USD/JPY 曾一度回測 157.50 附近，不
過在美日利差的主導下，USD/JPY 依舊保持偏多的走勢，終場收在 158.31，週線上
漲 84 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、路透短觀調查顯示，受半導
體強勁訂單推動，日本 10 月大型製造業信心升至正 22，創近五年新高；但原料成
本高漲重擊非製造業，信心指數降至正 23。與此同時，此前投下升息反對票的日銀
鴿派委員佐藤綾野立場軟化，表態支持分階段升息，市場對後續正常化預期升溫，
預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間 。二 、 日本首相高市早苗表示密切關注匯率與物價
走勢 ， 強調日本已脫離通縮 ， 將完全尊重央行政策獨立性 ， 暗示不反對進一步升息 。
面對通膨加劇與日圓走貶壓力，其過往堅持的貨幣寬鬆立場已出現轉向。然而目前
美日實質利差仍大，持續為 USD/JPY 提供支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，
USD/JPY 持續沿著上彎的月線上攻，然匯價持續承壓於季線下方，在有效站上季線
及突破前高壓力前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震
盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：157.10-159.50。

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 震盪 走跌 。週初， 因法國債券風險與區域政治不確定性 ，
EUR/USD 下行最低來到 1.1160； 週間 ，受中東地緣風險升溫與歐洲財政疑慮帶動避
險需求 ， 加上美債殖利率維持高檔 ， 美元利差優勢延續，歐元維持低檔； 臨近週末 ，
逢低歐元有買盤支撐，帶動歐元收復部分跌幅，終場收在 1.1201，週線下跌 46 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學
家連恩表示，夏末能源成本飆升為經濟增長帶來下行風險，並可能限制央行收緊貨
幣政策的力道，與此同時，歐元區 10 月 Sentix 投資者信心指數由 5.1 降至 2.7，德
國預期指數同步滑落，反映市場對經濟復甦的信心明顯降溫，預計將令歐元持續承
壓。二、法國社會動盪與支出訴求阻礙財政減赤進程，加劇債務可持續性疑慮，並
推升法德公債利差與信用風險，令歐元承壓走弱。相較美國經濟展現韌性且聯準會
維持偏鷹立場，歐洲深陷景氣放緩與財政掣肘，歐洲央行緊縮空間受限。在歐美基
本面與政策前景持續分歧下 ，EUR/USD 缺乏實質支撐 ， 後市將維持偏弱整理並下探
關鍵支撐。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖多次向下測試 1.1165 關
鍵價位均獲得支撐，惟每次反彈均無法站上五日線，且各天期均線下彎呈現空頭排
列。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1100-1.1260

                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.10.05-115.10.08 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.830%  
30 天期短票利率   1.66% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109     2.05% 
－ 5 年期- A15108 1.82%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週邁入新的提存期 ， 央行連日減發存單 ， 令市場資金供
給充裕，帶動短率朝區間低位移動。 
週一央行標售 364 天期定存單 1100 億元 ， 加權平均得標利率為1.602%， 低於
市場預期區間 1.61%-1.635%，較上期標售結果下跌 4.1 個基本點。 
週 四 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.68%，利率大致持平於上週。 
二、 受美債利率續創新高影響，週四 10 年期指標券 A15109 成交在 2.05%，創同
年期券自 2008 年以來高點，5 年期指標券 A15108 則未成交，最近成交利率
為 1.82%。 
台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨
警戒線 ， 且創兩年半來最大漲幅 ， 目前油價仍居高難下 ， 通膨壓力將令央行維
持偏緊的貨幣政策，加上國際債市利率持續走升，台債殖利率恐面臨上行壓
力，預估 5 年券區間為 1.85%~1.95%，10 年券區間為 2.0%~2.10%。

市場焦點
美國
• 9月ISM服務業PMI降至54.9，雖商業活動與新訂單放緩 ，但就業回升至 50.1、訂單積壓增加 ，
顯示服務需求仍具韌性；另一方面，價格指數升至74.0，創2022年7月以來新高，供應商交貨
速度亦放緩 ，反映燃料 、原物料及供應鏈成本升高 ，景氣未明顯轉弱之際 ，通膨壓力反而重
新升溫；同時全球長債殖利率同步走高 ，AI基建投資延續企業成長動能 ，也提高資本需求 。
整體而言 ，美國經濟尚未明顯轉弱 ，但通膨與長端利率壓力同步升高 ，使金融條件維持偏緊 。
中國
• 彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與 AI普及下，中國產業快速發展出現供給過剩 ，產業獲利
能力面臨挑戰，未來三年中國AI產業將可能處於虧損狀態，不過即使模型價格下降，AI使用量
增加將帶動Token需求大幅成長，其中具有企業客戶、專有資料及生態優勢的AI龍頭，將比單

純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。
黃金
• 歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵 ，加上長期通膨風險升溫 ，宏觀不確定性讓實體黃金
市場逆勢吸金 。本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易 ，實體黃金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中
期的支撐。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以投資組
合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。

美國9月ISM服務需求略降、成本壓力升高，景氣韌性與通膨風險並存
• 服務業維持擴張，需求略降但就業回穩：9月ISM服務業指數降至54.9(前月55.4)，連續27個月位於擴張區間；商業活動
指數降至56.5、新訂單降至 59.8，顯示服務需求較前月放緩 ，但就業指數回升至 50.1、重返擴張區間 ，訂單積壓升至
56.6，顯示內需仍具韌性，服務業尚未出現明顯轉弱訊號。
• 成本與供應鏈壓力升高 ，通膨降溫面臨干擾： 價格指數升至 74.0，創2022年7月以來新高 ，且為7個月來第6次高於70；
供應商交貨指數升至53.2，顯示交貨速度放緩，燃料、柴油、鋼材、銅及記憶體等成本上升，加上供應受限，使服務業
投入成本壓力擴大，通膨降溫進程面臨干擾。
• 市場觀察：美國服務業為經濟成長的重要支撐，目前經濟呈現「需求仍強、通膨升溫」的拉鋸格局，雖服務業景氣支撐

美國企業獲利，但成本上升可能推升利率波動，投資上宜偏向美國短中天期投資級債，兼顧較高收益率與利率風險控管；
股票則可聚焦具定價能力、獲利與現金流穩健的優質企業。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6 資料來源：ISM Services PMI Report， 2026.10.6
ISM服務業指數分項指標變動對比表ISM服務業PMI主要分項指數變化

全球利率同步走高、AI投資持續擴張，長端美債壓力仍高
• 全球長債殖利率同步走高，反映利率環境重新定價：美國財政部長貝森特指出，近期美債殖利率攀升與德國、日本等主
要債市同步上行，反映全球資本成本重新定價，而非市場對美國資產失去信心；加上目前尚未出現資金大規模撤離美債
並轉向其他主權債券的現象，因此債市波動較偏向全球利率環境調整，而非金融系統性風險。
• AI投資增加資金需求，支撐較高利率環境：貝森特亦駁斥AI泡沫論，認為微軟、Alphabet及Meta等大型科技企業仍以
實際獲利與現金流支撐持續擴大AI資本支出，不同於過去缺乏基本面的科技泡沫；龐大的資料中心 、電力與半導體投資
需求提高整體資金成本，也成為推升長天期利率的重要因素之一。
• 市場觀察：目前市場經濟近況屬於利率高檔、成長尚穩的情境中，因而投資策略建議配置大型科技股、AI基建供應鏈及

受惠利差環境的金融股具相對優勢；債券配置則以中短天期投資級債優先，避免長天期債券受利率上行壓力侵蝕。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.6
長端殖利率維持今年以來高檔
資料來源：兆豐銀行整理， 2026.10.6

• 美國8月貿易逆差擴大至2025年初以來最高水平：美國提高進口關稅後，貿易失衡問題仍未明顯改善，商務部數據顯示，8
月貿易逆差擴大13.7%至1,056億美元，且超出市場預期的1,021億美元。經通膨調整後，8月商品貿易逆差擴大至1,147億美
元，同樣為2025年3月以來最大。
• 觀察美國本土投資能否進一步轉化為出口能力：AI建設帶動半導體與設備進口，加上能源成本上升及關稅政策干擾，推升進
口額至歷史新高。不過，強勁內需也顯示消費與企業投資仍具支撐力。隨著第三季財報季即將展開，根據Factset數據，分析
師預期第三季標普500企業獲利年增約29.5%，加上企業獲利預期持續上修，強化投資人對高利率環境下企業基本面的信心。
投資策略上，相較於單筆投資，此時的高成長與高波動的環境，更適合分批布局的定期定額策略來參與市場。

• 實質薪資持續成長，內需復甦動能增強：日本8月實質現金收入年增1.5%，連續第8個月成長，名目工資與基本工資均年增
3.8%，顯示薪資成長已逐步抵銷通膨對購買力的侵蝕；剔除獎金、加班費及抽樣偏差後，全職勞工薪資仍成長2.8%，反映
薪資上漲具有一定持續性。實質所得改善有助家庭消費與內需回升，進一步支撐企業營收與獲利展望。
• 薪資與通膨並行，日股中長期仍具配置價值：薪資持續上升有助日本形成薪資增加、進而消費擴大，導向企業營收改善的正
向循環，同時也強化日本央行持續推進貨幣政策正常化的條件。雖然升息可能推升日圓與資金成本，但金融股可受惠於利差
擴大，且企業治理改革、AI投資及自動化需求仍提供獲利支撐。近期日股AI與半導體族群已明顯走強，顯示市場仍重視企業
獲利成長；中長期建議維持日股正向看法，聚焦金融、AI、機器人、自動化及電力設備等具結構性成長題材的企業。

中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境
• 中國AI產業陷入逐底競爭困境 ：彭博指出在政府積極推動開源策略與
AI普及下，中國AI產業快速發展 ，但也逐漸出現供給過剩 ，使產業獲
利能力面臨挑戰 。開源模式雖有助於降低技術門檻 、應用普及 ，但也
使技術優勢難以長期維持 ，加上中國市場長期習慣低價甚至免費的數
位服務，多數AI業者難以透過訂閱模式獲利，轉而依賴利潤較低的API
服務作為主要收入來源 。目前中國已備案大型語言模型 (LLM)數量達
988個，包括DeepSeek、Kimi及智譜AI等新創企業快速崛起，進一步
加劇市場競爭。此外API服務逐漸被視為基礎公用服務，即使AI Agent
帶動詞元(Token)需求快速成長，也未必能同步提升獲利，由於開源模
型可由不同雲端業者部署 ，市場進入門檻降低 ，業者更難建立長期競

爭優勢。
• 後市展望： 考量龐大資本支出與持續價格競爭 ，未來三年中國 AI產業
整體仍可能處於虧損狀態 ，市場將形成「美國閉源、高毛利」與「中
國開源 、低成本 」模式 ，即使模型價格下降 ，AI 使用量增加將帶動
Token需求大幅成長 ，進而推升資料中心 、雲端服務及 AI晶片需求 ，
從投資角度來看，基礎設施供應商(晶片、伺服器、資料中心、雲端)受
惠程度可能高於模型開發商；而具有企業客戶 、專有資料及生態優勢
的AI龍頭，將比單純提供模型服務的公司更具長期競爭力。

人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台，有望提振中國經濟
和市場信心
• 人行下調抵押補充貸款利率和房貸貼息等政策出台 ，有望提振中國經濟和市場信心 ：日前中國推出購房貸款貼息政策及下調
PSL(抵押補充貸款)利率，代表穩增長政策進一步加碼 ，有助於提振市場信心與經濟預期 ，此次政策不僅支持房地產市場 ，也
將「六張網」建設、科技創新、民營及小微企業 ，納入重點扶持範圍 ，透過增加再貸款額度 ，與提高支持比例 ，帶動投資、
就業及消費回升。短期A股有望在政策推動下出現築底反彈，其中基建投資、製造業投資、房市銷售及居民消費有機會改善，
同時PSL(抵押補充貸款)降息有助於流動性環境邊際寬鬆 ，降低市場對經濟增長的疑慮 ，後市來看企業獲利與信用環境有望逐
步修復，進一步鞏固A股慢牛格局。投資建議上房貸貼息政策有利房地產、建材、家居等產業鏈，金融業受惠資金成本下降與

信貸回升，食品飲料、零售、家電及服務消費則可望受惠於居民消費意願提升。
資料來源：華金證券，2026.10.5

中國、日本減持美國公債的背後邏輯與差異
• 橋水基金創始人達利歐警告 ，美國公債正面臨中日兩國減持的風險 ，數據顯示這項警告已成事實 。2026年前七個月，中國
美國公債持倉減少660億美元至6,180億美元；同期日本持倉亦減少820億美元至1.1兆美元，扣除價格變動的估值效應後，
兩國實質持倉均大幅下滑。然而，兩國削減美國公債的背後動機並不相同，這將導致未來的減持軌跡出現明顯分化。
• 中國戰略性長期去美元化：1.降低地緣政治風險與轉向資產多元化 ，隨著人民幣在國際貿易結算中使用增加 ，市場流動性提
升，中國對美元儲備依賴度顯著下降。2.美債財政可持續性隱憂，美國政府債務持續攀升，引發對美國長期財政健康疑慮。
3.根據彭博經濟研究模型預測，在漸進式減持情境下，中國的美元儲備(以美債與機構債為主)到2050年可能減少約50%。

• 中國搭配戰術性節奏調整 (短期緩和因素)：儘管長期趨勢向下 ，但目前中美利差顯著 (美中30年期公債利差超過 350個基點)，
在美債殖利率維持高位期間，過快減持意味著實現債券價格下跌的虧損，且可能加劇人民幣升值壓力。因此，中國短中期內
傾向採取「戰略性減持、戰術性放緩」的靈活步調。
美國公債持倉：中國 vs 日本

日本受政策利率與本土資本回流驅動
• 與中國的結構性考量不同，日本的減持主要出自週期性因素與匯率干預需求。
• 日本央行貨幣政策轉鷹： 日本央行正加快升息腳步 。隨著日債殖利率上揚 (30年期日債殖利率升至約 4.2%)，美日利差持續
收窄，吸引日本國內機構資金回流本土主權債市場。
• 匯率干預政策：日本為防範日圓過度貶值，日本財務省傾向出售部分海外美債資產以籌集外匯干預資金。若日圓再次面臨壓
力，此類拋售干預可能持續。因此，隨著日本央行逐步走向政策正常化，日本對美債的持倉大概會延續下滑軌跡。
• 小結與投資建議：美國公債作為全球無風險資產的地位正面臨結構性挑戰，中國的「長期去美元化」與日本的「本土利率回
升資金回流」將形成雙重壓力，這意味著美債殖利率短期內難大幅下行，美國財政部發債成本居高不下，進而推高全球資本

的融資成本。投資債券配置建議採取「中短天期＋階梯式配置」策略，長天期美國公債(20年以上)受發債供給過剩及中日兩
國長期減持影響，價格波動較大。建議投資人適度降低長天期美國公債暴險，轉向防禦性較佳的中短天期美國公債或高品質
高評級公司債，以鎖定當前較高殖利率同時控管利率風險。
殖利率差：美元與人民幣 vs 美元與日圓

黃金市場資金流向分歧，實體投資有望支撐中長期金價表現
• 利率與匯率因素，壓抑金價近期表現：9月以來，美國公債殖利率飆升、美元指數走揚，由於黃金本身不孳息，資金傾向轉
往固定收益市場，疊加美元走強壓抑國際購買意願，金價自8月底高點至今已下修約11%。
• 9月資金流向分歧，實體投資強、期貨市場弱：根據世界黃金協會統計，9月在金價下跌之際，全球實體黃金ETF卻吸引大量
資金流入，流入約100億美元(67噸)。另一方面，COMEX黃金期貨市場投機部位大幅下降 ，避險基金與其他機構投資人的
多頭部位明顯減少，造成期貨市場賣壓增加，進一步推動金價下跌。
• 實體黃金市場逆勢吸金反應投資人的避險需求：歐美國家財政可持續性的疑慮持續發酵，加上美伊衝突未解及AI算力需求強
勁，長期通膨風險升溫，宏觀的不確定性讓實體黃金市場逆勢吸金。從數據上來看，本波下跌主要反映去槓桿交易，實體黃

金的基本面未變 ，有望為金價帶來中期的支撐 。建議投資人逢修正分批進場 ，可透過黃金存摺或是實體黃金 ETF(GLD)，以
投資組合多元化的角度布局。波段投機者，從技術指標來看，可留意4,000美元關卡是否出現支撐。
資料來源：世界黃金協會，2026.10.7。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良

8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。

營收走勢圖

研究員/王建智 MICHAELWANG82121188@UNI-
PSG.COM

研究部對於長榮看法轉趨正向，係因：(1) 3Q26 受惠貨櫃航運
旺季及運價回升，平均運價漲幅明顯高於燃油成本增幅， 帶動獲
利顯著成長。(2) 4Q26 雖進入傳統淡季，惟北美農曆春節前備
貨 、歐洲貨量成長及亞洲區域航線旺季提供支撐，預期獲利雖低
於 3Q26，但仍優於 2Q26。(3) 2027 年雖面臨新船交付增加的供
給壓力， 但全球碼頭擴建速度落後船隊成長，加上塞港及紅海繞
航持續消耗有效運力，有助減緩新增供給對運價的衝擊， 北美及
歐洲航線長約運價亦預期高於 2026 年，支撐全年獲利。預估
2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 分別為45.21 元（+42.7%YoY）／27.89 元
（-38.3%YoY）。歷史本淨比區間為 0.4～1.8 倍，以 2027 年底
預估每股淨值 298.36 元為評價基礎，給予0.95 倍本淨比， 目標
價 283 元，將投資評等調升至買進。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 運價漲幅高於燃油成本增幅，帶動獲利顯著成長 
長榮 3Q26 營收 1,467.76 億元，+39.6%QoQ/+51.4%YoY，主要受
惠第三季貨櫃航運傳統旺季， 歐美航線進入零售商備貨高峰， 帶
動貨運需求回升，加上紅海繞航、 全球港口壅塞持續消耗有效運
力，以及航商透過艙位控管調節供給，推升主要航線運價。7、8
月平均運費分別為 1,539 及1,577 美元／TEU， 參考9 月SCFI 月
均 3,657 點，+8.0%MoM， 推估9 月平均運費約 1,703 美元／TEU，
並假設 9 月貨量與 8 月持平，則 3Q26 平均運費約 1,606 美元／
TEU，+44.7%QoQ，貨量276 萬 TEU，-1.8%QoQ，營收成長主要由
運價上漲貢獻。

成本方面 ， 預估每噸耗油成本由2Q26 的 645 美元升至 714 美元 ，
+10.6%QoQ，假設季度耗油量持平，燃油費用增加約22 億元，惟
運價漲幅明顯高於燃油成本增幅 ， 營收增加帶來的獲利貢獻可望
大幅超過成本上升影響。預估 3Q26 稅後淨利 479.69 億元，
+199.2%QoQ/+120.6%YoY，稅後EPS 22.16 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

圖一、各月平均運費、每噸耗油成本、貨量

資料來源：長榮，統一投顧整理 
2. 4Q26 進入傳統淡季，惟區域航線需求支撐營運 
第四季為貨櫃航運傳統淡季， 整體需求不如第三季旺季強勁， 惟
根據訂艙及裝載情況 ， 至11 月前貨量仍具支撐 。 北美航線方面 ，
美國主要港口進口貨櫃量維持高檔，零售庫存銷售比低於 1.3，
顯示零售商庫存水位相對偏低，仍有持續補貨需求，預期 11 月
貨量隨淡季趨緩 ，12 月則有農曆春節前備貨需求支撐 。 歐洲航線
方面 ，2026 年貨量成長優於預期 ， 雖運價自7 月後逐步回落 ， 但
紅海地緣政治風險尚未解除， 繞航仍持續消耗有效運力。 亞洲區
域航線第四季進入傳統旺季 ， 受惠中國加一帶動東南亞及南亞原
料、半成品運輸需求成長。此外，全球塞港造成約300 萬至 400
萬 TEU 有效運力損失，占全球總運力約 11%至 12%，搭配航商艙
位控管，有助支撐淡季運價。

預估 4Q26 營收 1,174.21 億元，-20.0%QoQ/+37.0%YoY，稅後淨
利 255.68 億元，-46.7%QoQ/+200.2%YoY，稅後 EPS 11.81 元。

3. 2027 年新船交付壓力增加，港口壅塞限制有效運力供給 
2027~2029 年為全球貨櫃船新船交付高峰，目前全球貨櫃船總運
力約3,400 萬TEU，至2030 年待交付新船運力超過1,400 萬TEU，
主流研調機構(Clarksons、Drewry)預估 2027 年名目運力供給增
速為 7.7~7.9%，供給成長壓力仍是 2027 年主要風險。惟名目運
力增加不代表有效運力等幅成長 ， 過去十年全球碼頭處理能力年
均成長約 2.7%，低於船隊運力年均成長約 5.4%，港口基礎設施
擴張速度落後船隊成長，塞港問題短期難以改善。紅海及蘇伊士
運河恢復通行亦為重要變數， 歐洲航線若恢復通行，單程約節省

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

7 天 、 往返約14 天 ， 但船舶集中抵港可能加劇碼頭壅塞 ， 抵銷部
分航程縮短釋出的運力。長榮截至 2030 年尚有 72 艘新船待交
付 ， 合計運力約89 萬 TEU， 並持續投資重要樞紐碼頭 ， 提升靠泊
效率及船隊調度彈性。

4. 2027 年歐美長約運價預期高於 2026 年，支撐全年獲利 
2027 年北美及歐洲航線長約運價預期均高於 2026 年，主要受惠
三項因素 。 首先 ，2026 年第三季貨櫃運價大幅上漲 ， 目前現貨運
價明顯高於前一年度長約議價時的水準 ， 為新年度合約價格調升
奠定基礎 。 其次 ， 全球港口處理能力擴張速度落後船隊運力成長 ，
加上紅海繞航及主要港口壅塞持續消耗有效運力 ， 航商供給調節
能力提升，有利維持長約議價能力。最後，歐美航線貨運需求仍
具支撐 ， 北美零售商庫存水位偏低 ， 歐洲航線貨量成長優於預期 ，
提供長約價格上調條件。歐洲航線新約自 2027 年初開始適用，
北美航線則自 5 月起生效 ， 預期新年度長約運價調升將逐步反映
於營收，並有助減緩現貨運價波動對獲利的影響，支撐 2027 年
營運表現。

5. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 45.21/27.89 元，塞港及長約運
價調升支撐獲利高於歷史平均水準 
預估 2026/2027 年營收分別為 4,558.69／4,178.70 億元，分別
+20.3%YoY/-8.3%YoY，稅後淨利分別為 978.74／603.81 億元，
分別+42.7%YoY/-38.3%YoY，稅後 EPS 分別為 45.21 元／27.89
元。2026 年受惠第三季運價大幅上漲 ， 帶動全年獲利回升 。2027
年雖面臨新船交付增加及運價自高檔回落的壓力 ， 惟全球碼頭處
理能力成長落後船隊運力，加上塞港及紅海繞航限制有效供給，
歐美航線長約價格亦預期高於 2026 年 ， 有助減緩獲利下滑幅度 。

For the month of October 2026, we estimate AirTAC’s monthly revenue will decrease 
to Rmb729mn (-10% MoM, +16% YoY)/NT$3,447mn (-10% MoM, +28% YoY) 
We expect October revenue will go down by 10% MoM to Rmb729mn/NT$3,447mn, 
mainly due to 19% fewer working days in Mainland China. However, we expect to see 
improvement in daily pneumatic revenue (Rmb32.3mn in Oct vs. Rmb 29.4mn in Sept) as 
the improvement in daily pneumatic revenue is driven by the strong growth from key 
customers, including electronics, followed by machine tools and battery, and we further 
expect demand to improve following the end of low season.

Earnings and TP revisions 
We revise down our 2026E earnings estimates by 1% to factor in actual 3Q26 revenue 
(revise down 2026 revenue by 1%). We revise down our 2027E/2028E earnings estimate 
by 1%/1% to factor in a more conservative assumption on battery and linear guide 
business (revise down AirTAC revenue by 1%/1% in 2027E/2028E). We continue to see 
the company focusing on cost control as well as experiencing a better product mix 
outlook, but we expect some offset by rising material cost (revise down 2026E/2028E 
OPM by 0.1/0.1ppt).

### Page 3

Maintain our Buy rating on AirTAC, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925), 
based on an unchanged target P/E of 26x on 2027 EPS.

Investment view: Accelerating monthly YoY from smaller segments could be a surprise. 
Maintain positive view on AirTAC 
We reiterate our Buy rating on AirTAC as we continue to believe the company is a key 
beneficiary of the factory automation/industrial automation localization trend in 
mainland China, and we expect the company will also benefit from the prolonged 
upcycle supported by the ongoing government stimulus policies. We see multiple key 
end-segments’ performance tracking ahead of the company’s 2026 full year guidance of 
20%+ YoY growth (see here), with battery/textile/machine tools/electronics delivering 
55%/46%/41%/23% YTD YoY growth while total revenue in Rmb is tracking at 23% YTD 
YoY growth. 
We continue to see solid monthly YoY growth from smaller segments, such as energy 
lighting and textile. However, the battery segment, which is the key growth driver in 
2025 (with 89% YoY in 2025), is seeing its first negative monthly YoY growth since Oct 
2024, while other major contribution sectors, such as electronics and automotive are 
seeing decelerating monthly YoY growth. As a result, we remain cautious on the 
potential of exaggerated orders (including advance or duplicate orders) despite new 
orders intake continuing to outpace shipments, and we will continue to monitor the 
underlying end-demand closely.

### Page 4

Maintain Buy on AirTAC with a lower 12m TP of NT$1,895 (from NT$1,925). 
Investment Thesis - AirTAC 
AirTAC is the 2nd-largest pneumatic supplier in mainland China with 28% market share 
in 2025, outpacing most local competitors, owing to its low-cost yet high quality 
product, and service quality reputation in most key cities in mainland China. We forecast 
its market share will reach 30% in 2026E and expect AirTAC to be the structural winner 
in a deflationary market, due to its: (1) strong market growth and leadership: AirTAC 
continues to expand its market share in mainland China driven by competitive pricing 
that is more than 10% lower vs. international peers (with 45%+ GM / 30-35% OPM) and 
high demand elasticity among mainland China customers; and (2) superior cost structure 
and financial health: with best-in-class cash cost and a solid balance sheet, featuring 
positive net cash over FCF since 2023/2024, we believe AirTAC is well-equipped to 
weather industry downturns and capitalize on market restructuring. 
Overall, we believe AirTAC will see better revenue/profitability in the long term given its 
share gainer position in the China pneumatic market and potential to gain share in the 
linear guide market (improved demand in the next 1-2 years). This should lead to 
strengthened valuation, vs. its recent trading range (trading below its 10-year avg. P/E 
multiple). We are Buy rated on AirTAC. 
Price target risks and methodology - AirTAC 
Valuation methodology: Our 12m TP of NT$1,895 is based on a target P/E of 26.0x on 
our 2027E EPS (0.25x STDV above avg of the last pneumatic upcycle). 
Key downside risks: (1) Stronger competition from peers; (2) sharper-than-expected 
Rmb depreciation, and (3) a longer-than-expected end-market demand downtrend in 
mainland China.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 12 Oct 2026 close.

return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

### Page 1

ASE Technology Holding

3Q26 sales outperformance affirms solid 
transformation progress

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 900.00 TWD | Price: 762.00 TWD

3Q26 sales beat on LEAP ramp and mature ASP strength 
ASE reported 3Q26 sales +28% QoQ better than its guide, driven by stronger IC ATM 
(+20% QoQ vs. BofAe +13% QoQ) and mild upside in EMS (+42% QoQ vs. BofAe +40% 
QoQ). We believe IC ATM strength is led by modest mainstream packaging and testing 
price hike on industry supply tightness, supplemented by LEAP business ramp. EMS also 
benefited from Apple product seasonal builds and higher component cost pass through. 
With improving packaging and testing utilization and ASP and rising LEAP mix, we 
expect its IC ATM GMs to improve 300bps QoQ to 30% (vs. high-20% guide) though 
consolidated GMs (+180bps QoQ to 23%) could be partially offset by EMS outgrowth. 
AI pipeline diversification anchors capex plan in ’26-28 
We model ASE’s 4Q26 IC ATM sales +7-9% QoQ as its LEAP opportunity diversifying 
from TSMC outsourcing to direct project wins including AMD’s Venice CPU, putting sales 
on track to +42% YoY in ’26 (vs. its +35% guide). Its IC ATM GMs could further expand 
to 31% as LEAP reaching 30% of its sales (vs. 18%/22% in 1H/3Q26). While we expect 
ASE to spend US$13bn+ annual capex in ’27-28 (vs. US$10.5bn in ’26), the accelerating 
IC ATM sales (BofAe +40% CAGR from ’26-28) on advanced expansion and mature price 
uplift, along with the business mix upgrade to cloud AI/HPC projects carrying 35-45% 
GMs should offset hiking depreciation (BofAe +39% CAGR from ’26-28), lifting its IC 
ATM GM from 29% in ’26 to low 30% in ’27 and mid 30% in ’28 with operating leverage. 
Stronger earnings quality unlocks re-rating potential 
We lift our ’26/27/28E EPS to NT$20/NT$34/NT$50 on strong LEAP outlook. Keep Buy 
rating and raise POs from NT$800 (US$51) to NT$900 (US$58) still on 20x 2H27-1H28 
P/E (vs. its 5-26x range) backed by its structural business transformation with better 
sales and GMs growth profile as the industry supply further consolidates amid higher 
tech entry barrier.

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Established in 1984 and headquartered in Taiwan, ASE 
Technology Holding is the no.1 outsourced assembly and 
testing (OSAT) firm globally, in terms of market share by 
revenue. ASE acquired Siliconware Precision (SPIL) in 2018, 
and had acquired USI in 2010, which became its EMS 
business segment providing synergies in system-level 
packaging. 2 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
We rate ASE Buy, factoring in the company's opportunity in 
advanced packaging and testing evidenced in TSMC's 
outsourcing and its own heavy capex investment. The 
strategy shift should allow the company to capture fast 
growing addressable market in high performance 
computing applications and improves its margins. ASE 
could maintain its leading position in the Outsourced 
Semiconductor Assembly and Test (OSAT) universe, owing 
to its well-established footprints and widespread service 
offerings.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
ASE Technology Holding (XSRIF / ASX) 
We value ASE Technology Holding at NT$900 per share (US$58 for ADR), based on 20x 
2H27-1H28E P/E, above its historical range of 5-26x, in view of its solidified industry 
position in AI era. We use P/E ratio to value ASE as the company has been able to 
maintain healthy profitability through the cycles in the past 20 years due to 
consolidating industry landscape supporting a more benign supply/demand and keeping 
pricing and margins less cyclical.

Downside risks to our PO are 1) share loss and/or ASP erosion owing to Chinese players' 
cannibalization and/or ASE's worsening execution, 2) weakening end demand due to 
uncontrollable matters, such as macro halt and/or geopolitical tensions, 3) regulatory 
issues that could hinder ASE from generating synergies and benefit its competitors.

營收走勢圖

研究員/張均壕 JUN_HAO1@uni-psg.com

研究部對拓凱未來營運看法轉趨正向 ，主要是考量：(1)占營收比
重最大的自行車業務在 2H26 出現改善，隨著主力客戶新品銷售表
現較佳，帶動訂單增加，預期 2027 年自行車業務有望止跌回溫。
(2)航太、安全帽及其他類業務接單保持強勁，其中航太受惠於航
空座椅需求增加，安全帽則有高階產品及新客戶訂單貢獻，加上精
密塑件業務持續擴張 ， 將有助於優化產品組合 。(3) 2Q26 受子公司
一次性費用及匯率等因素干擾，毛利率與獲利下滑，但隨下半年出
貨規模擴大、產品組合改善，預期 2H26 獲利能力將逐步回升。研
究部預估拓凱 2026 年 EPS 13.52 元(-9%YOY)，2027 年EPS 15.28
元(+13%YOY)。因公司營運重回成長軌道，且產品組合改善有利於
中長期獲利表現，故以 2027 年獲利為評價基礎，給予 12 倍本益
比，目標價 183 元，投資評等由中立調升至買進。

重點摘要：

1. 主力客戶新車種上市，自行車業務回溫 ： 
拓凱營收約有 50% 來自碳纖維自行車車架與組件，最大核心
客戶為全球知名自行車 品牌-Specialized。雖然公司的自行
車業務在1H26衰退近二成，但受惠主力客戶 年中新品上市並
在終端市場銷售亮眼 ， 客戶對拓凱積極追加車架 、 輪圈與前叉
等複材組件訂單 ， 帶動拓凱近期自行車業務顯著改善 。 在去年
下半年低基期與今年新車拉貨潮帶動下，預計 2026全年自行
車營收減幅將收斂至個位數（研究部估減少8%YoY）。2027年
隨客戶新品需求與庫存去化完畢，預 估自行車業務營收將能
回溫，成長3%YoY。

2. 安全帽、航太及其他類業務展望正向，成長動能強勁： 
自行車以外業務為拓凱營運成長的核心動能 。 其中 ， 拓凱的安
全帽業務近年出貨增加 ， 且產品結構大幅改善 ， 高階產品占比
已達2/3，預計帶動今年安全帽營收大幅增長30%YoY，且因引
進電子智能安全帽新客戶，預期明年該業務營收仍可成長
7%YoY。球拍業務方面，今年出貨暢旺，預期今年球拍營收能
創下歷史新高 ， 全年度球拍營收預計成長18%YoY。 不過 ， 因近
兩年基期拉高，明年看法較謹慎，預計小幅衰退5%YoY。航太
醫療業務方面 ， 航太接單展望樂觀 ， 預期今年該類別營收增長
14%YoY，且1Q27起新增飛機座椅訂單將正式投產 ， 預計明年該
類別營收將再有12%YoY的增速 。 另外 ， 歸類在其他類之子公司

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

新鴻洲部分，受惠自行車煞把手、電子TOF精密塑件等產品出
貨增加 ， 營運動能依舊偏強 ， 預計該業務2026年、2027年營收
分別成長23%、13%YoY。

3. 一次性負面因素淡化，3Q26毛利率與獲利將回升： 
回顧拓凱2Q26財報 ， 受提列子公司一次性費用的衝擊 ， 導致單
季毛利率下滑至31.3%(季減3個百分點，年減6.7個百分點)，
加上業外有逾6千萬元的匯損，拖累季度獲利表現，2Q26稅後
淨利2.67億元(-19%QoQ，-5%YoY)，2Q26EPS下滑至2.94元。然
而 ， 此費用屬於非經常性的短期會計調整 ， 本業高階複材競爭
力並未受損 。 下半年隨自行車業務改善 ， 拓凱3Q26合併營收回
升至27.16億元(+6%QoQ，+12%YoY)。 此外 ， 由於一次性因素淡
化 ， 加上高階安全帽 、 航太及其他類產品出貨增加 ， 產品組合
優化，研究部預估拓凱3Q26毛利率將回升至33.8%，營業利益
5.41億元(+37%QoQ，+13%YoY)，稅後淨利3.55億元(+33%QoQ，
+1%YoY)，3Q26EPS 3.91元，獲利將較第2季明顯改善。

4. 2026年營收近百億元，預估2026年EPS 13.52元： 
受惠於安全帽 、 球拍 、 航太及其他業務成長 ， 抵銷自行車業務
的衰退 ， 預期拓凱全年營收可望接近百億元 ， 研究部預估拓凱
2026年合併營收99.73億元(+8%YoY)。不過，由於2Q26受一次
性費用及匯率等因素干擾，今年度毛利率預估降至33.1%，營
業利益16.91億元(-2%YoY)，稅後淨利12.28億元(-9%YoY)，
EPS 13.52元。

5. 各業務成長帶動獲利回升，預估2027年EPS 15.28元： 
展望2027年 ， 隨自行車業務恢復成長 ， 航太及安全帽新訂單持
續貢獻，以及其他類業務穩健擴張，研究部預估 2027年營收
104.34億元(+5%YoY)， 毛利率回升至33.8%， 營業利益18.50億
元(+9%YoY)，稅後淨利13.88億元(+13%YoY)，EPS 15.28元。

6. 越南產能持續擴充，並積極佈局新應用商機 ： 
因應客戶分散生產基地與供應鏈調整需求，拓凱持續擴充越
南複合材料產品線 ， 目前除自行車前叉 、 車架等零組件外 ， 亦
逐步增加航空座椅零組件的生產，未來航太板材及球拍等產
品亦有機會導入越南製造 。 公司也已著手規劃越南二期廠房 ，
強化海外生產彈性 。 此外 ， 中科后里新廠除作為營運總部及研
發據點外 ， 也將支援新業務開發 。 初期鎖定人形機器人關鍵的
腰部與膝蓋複材結構件與客戶進行共同開發，同時也積極切
入農業與商用無人機 、 低軌衛星等先端應用 。 子公司新鴻洲方

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

面 ， 則運用精密射出及模具技術 ， 計畫開拓半導體與人形機器
人零件等應用 。 雖然新事業短期營收貢獻仍有限 ， 但隨產品開
發與客戶認證推進，長期有助於拓展複合材料及精密零組件
的應用領域。

個股聚焦  
2026/10/20

陳奕均 fion.chen@sinopac.com

泰合 (6467 TT) 
開花結果在即

永豐觀點 
抗血栓口溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議買進：泰合 10/20 上市掛牌，掛牌價105.6 元。由於(1) 泰合開發之口溶
膜劑型藥物有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點下，市場競爭力足；(2) 抗血栓口
溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即，2027 年在授權與上市權利金開始認列下 ，營運轉
虧為盈且呈大跳躍狀；(3) 由於多數學名藥廠受限專利訴訟，最快需至 2028 年 4
月後才可上市銷售，促使泰合享有約一年的「黃金市場空窗期」 ，能與原廠直接瓜
分市場。且因 BEQIA 屬 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥，即便2Q28 後學名藥陸續進場，其
獨特的劑型優勢仍能築起價格防護牆，所面臨的價折壓力相對輕微，建議買進，目
標價 170 元( 18 X 2027 EPS )。

營運現況與分析 
公司簡介：泰合生技成立於 2010 年，專注開發 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥與特殊學名
藥，核心技術為自主研發的 TDS 透皮／透黏膜傳輸平台，將現有口服藥品成分改
造為透皮與透黏膜藥物傳導劑型 ，提供方便使用且更安全的新劑型藥物。 產品開發
上，505(b)(2)部分，治療抗血栓口溶膜貼片(每日兩片)BEQIA 靜待歐美取證中外，
其餘則仍在臨床階段中，困難學名藥部分，化療止吐口溶膜2018 年日本取證銷售
中外，其餘四項藥品則在進行洽談授權合作。 
圖一：泰合產品線開發時程

資料來源：泰合；永豐投顧研究部整理，Oct. 2026

### Page 2

2

口溶膜劑型藥物能有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點 ：傳統口服錠劑藥物必須經
過食道、進入胃腸道消化，再經由肝臟代謝後才進入血液循環，泰合開發之口溶膜
劑型藥物， 可讓藥膜在接觸舌頭或口腔黏膜數秒內快速溶解 ， 病患完全不需飲水即
可完成給藥， 且部份藥物分子能直接透過口腔黏膜上皮細胞微血管吸收， 直接進入
全身血液循環， 不僅能提高藥物生體可用率， 還能減少胃酸對藥物的破壞與對胃腸
道的刺激下，有效解決高齡與吞嚥困難之臨床痛點，提高患者服藥順從性。 
抗血栓口溶膜貼片 BEQIA 歐美取證在即，提前卡位下，營運動能可期：泰合自主
開發的抗血栓口溶膜新劑型新藥 BEQIA， 預計4Q26 取得歐盟藥證 、2Q27 獲美國
FDA 核准上市。目前該藥原廠在美國市場年銷售額>200 億美元，相較原廠口服錠
劑，泰合 BEQIA 具備三大競爭優勢：不需飲水（服藥便利）、解決吞嚥困難（減
輕照顧端壓力），以及攜帶與給藥便利（優化長照與醫療機構給藥流程）。 
市場競爭方面，由於多數學名藥廠受限專利訴訟，最快需至2028 年 4 月後才可上
市銷售，促使泰合享有約一年的「黃金市場空窗期」 ，能與原廠直接瓜分市場。此
外，由於BEQIA 屬 505(b)(2)新劑型新藥，即便2Q28 後學名藥陸續進場，其獨特
的劑型優勢仍能築起價格防護牆，所面臨的折價壓力應相對輕微。 
泰合海外市場營銷預計將採授權分潤模式，研究部預估 1H27 歐美地區簽約金應
有機會各達 500~1000 萬美金外，隨美國地區 2Q27 上市、通路鋪設及醫保陸續
准入，Peak sales 市佔率 2-3%(預期將落於 2029-30 年)、銷售分潤 12-15%估算
下，稅後 EPS 貢獻將落於 10-20 元。 
財務預估：預估泰合 2026 年營收 0.1 億元，稅後虧損 1.89 億元，稅後 EPS 以掛
牌後股本 5.99 億元估算，稅後 EPS-3.15 元，2027 年預估歐美地區授權簽約金各
500 萬美金及 1000 萬美金 ， 及上市銷售後權利金認列下 ，預估營收將跳升至7.75
億元，稅後淨利 5.68 億元，稅後 EPS 9.49 元。

현대차 (005380)

EARNINGS PREVIEW Company Analysis | 자동차/로봇 | 2026.10.16  
4분기 반등 기대

3Q26 Preview 
현대차는 3Q26E 매출액 42.4조원(-9.4% YoY, -13.9% QoQ), 영업이익 2.5조원(-
2.8% YoY, -13.5% QoQ, OPM 5.8%)을 기록, 컨센서스를 하회할 전망이다. 각 부문
별 영업이익은 자동차 1.7조원(-2.0% YoY, -14.9% QoQ, OPM 4.8%), 금융 5,501억
원(-4.5% YoY, -27.5% QoQ, OPM 6.9%)으로 예상한다. 
생산 정상화 및 신차 확대를 통한 수익성 개선 기대 
현대차는 글로벌 도매 판매 91.8만대(-11.5% 이하 YoY), 중국 제외 판매량은 90.9
만대(-9.6%)를 기록했다. 7~8월에 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생산 차
질이 발생했다. 파업 영향으로 HEV 판매 증가율이 1%에 그치면서, HEV 판매 확대에 
따른 믹스 개선 효과도 시장 기대에 미치지 못했다.  
4분기부터는 3분기 파업 영향으로 지연된 생산량 회복 및 신차 확대가 본격화되면서, 
전분기 대비 매출 및 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 동사는 보급형 전기차 아이오닉3의 튀
르키예 생산을 9월부터 시작했으며, 이를 통해 유럽 시장 판매량은 점차 회복될 전망
이다. 또한, 1) HMGMA를 통한 HEV 미국 현지 생산 확대, 2) 제네시스 HEV 모델 순
차적 출시를 통해 내년부터 북미를 중심으로 믹스 개선이 본격화될 전망이다. 
투자의견은 Buy를 유지하고 목표주가는 520,000원으로 하향 
2027년 영업이익은 11.2조원(+10.2%)를 기록할 것으로 예상된다. 원화 약세 지속을 
반영해 이익 추정치를 하향하며, 이에 따라 목표주가를 하향한다. 다만, 현재 주가는 
12M Fwd P/E 기준 6.1배로 Legacy OEM 평균(7.7배)보다 낮은 수준이다. 1) RMAC 
성과 구체화, 2) RA(로봇 생산법인) 착공 등 Physical AI 사업이 구체화될 경우 밸류에
이션 할증이 가능할 전망이다. 
 Analyst 이병근 
bglee@ls-sec.co.kr

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• The recent tendency of some firms to bundle into prices
card-acceptance costs that had previously been surcharged
– and then some, in some cases – demonstrates a ‘cost-
plus’ approach to pricing. To the extent they can pass on
these costs through to prices, it suggests that demand was

strong enough to bear it. The RBA will take this as a signal
that demand is still outstripping supply.
• This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. Base wages
growth has been moderate for some years, though the
above-average increase in award wages this year will add
a little. Not all firms will seek to pass these cost increases
on, but some will, including because they have limited
scope to absorb the cost increase into margins.
• A cost-plus mentality has drawbacks, including that it

limits the imagination away from finding alternative,
efficiency-improving responses. It also accords with
recent academic research suggesting that firms form
expectations and set prices according to what is
happening in their own industry and experience, while
putting little weight on economy-wide inflation or central
bank credibility. This is an unpleasant message for many
consumers or anyone in an interest-sensitive sector when
supply shocks abound.

The recent banning of card surcharges has caused
considerable upset this month, even though it is a reversion
to historical and international practice. The card schemes
used to impose “no-surcharge” rules, until the RBA banned
these in 2003, and many other countries still have them. The
turnaround represents the RBA throwing in the towel on its
longstanding effort to use price incentives to get people to
choose low-cost payment methods. Surcharges no longer

made sense in a largely cashless economy. In any case, least-
cost routing puts the power to choose lower-cost methods in
the hands of the merchants who actually bear the cost.
In most cases, firms will simply roll the surcharge into the sticker
price, leaving the total cost to the consumer unchanged, just
more transparent. We also understand that some large retailers
have absorbed the cost – if they were surcharging in the first
place, which many did not. (The RBA estimated that around

16% of businesses imposed surcharges.) Inevitably, though,
some firms have lifted their prices by more than any reasonable
estimate of the merchant service fee that the surcharge was
meant to cover. And because this is Australia, of course takeaway
coffee has been the centre of consumer discontent.
Higher prices for one’s caffeine fix was unlikely to be the main
instigator of this month’s slump in consumer sentiment. Petrol
‘Cost-Plus’ costs
Forecast changes

• A full update to our forecasts was published in our October
Market Outlook  on Thursday 15 October.
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
prices are typically more salient for cost-of-living concerns, and
the before-and-after picture on the RBA announcement that week
shows that higher interest rates also contributed to the decline.
In some senses, though, the post-surcharge prices and the
RBA’s decisions are linked: businesses passing on increased

costs into their own prices – and expecting to be able to
make that increase stick – is exactly the kind of inflationary
environment the RBA is leaning against. Firms have been
subjected to an array of non-labour cost escalations, from
pandemic-era supply chain issues to electricity to fuel to
water to building supplies. And IT equipment prices, which
for decades were a reliable source of cost reductions, are
nowadays flat to up. Passing these costs on, leaving margins

intact, demonstrates that demand is not weak enough to
prevent this, and signals that firms’ inflationary expectations
might be higher than the RBA desires. The RBA will take this
as a sign that demand is still outstripping supply.
This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. While the Fair
Work Commission did hand down an above-average increase in
award wages this year and this was probably passed through
to prices in hospitality and other labour-intensive sectors, base

wages growth has been moderate for some years. Business
surveys point to non-labour cost escalation being the real issue
lately. This is why we were puzzled by the passage in the RBA
minutes this week suggesting that cost pressures reported
in business surveys might imply that the unemployment rate
consistent with inflation at target (the NAIRU) has risen. It is
a convenient belief, though, if unemployment is starting to
overshoot your forecasts, as the latest labour market data have

done compared with the RBA’s August forecasts.
Not all firms can pass on cost increases. Most small firms do
not have the market power, though coffee may be an exception
because convenience induces a kind of local market power. And
as noted above, large firms with everyday-low-price strategies
will instead try to find savings elsewhere in the supply chain.
Still, some firms will seek to pass these cost increases on.
Some observers might see this as corporate greed, but another

way to frame this strategy is simply as a ‘cost-plus’ mentality.
“Entrenched expectations that cost increases
can and should be passed on are precisely
what tight monetary policy is leaning against,
but only indirectly."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
It turns out that cost-plus pricing is especially common in
areas where regulators determine the price – water and other
utilities being examples where costs and prices have escalated
a lot in recent years.
There are limits to firms’ ability to absorb costs, too. The

failures of so many building firms a few years ago, squeezed
between post-pandemic cost escalation, delivery delays and
fixed-price contracts, were a case in point. Normally, there is
a contractual response, and it need not end in insolvency. The
episode does illustrate, however, that it is unrealistic to expect
firms never to pass cost increases through to prices.
The cost-plus mentality does have its drawbacks. It limits
the imagination away from finding responses that might save

costs elsewhere to absorb the cost hike, and in so doing, boost
efficiency. It was equally limiting in times of soft demand: in
the years before the pandemic, many firms professed a belief
that, because they couldn’t get price increases to stick, they
could not possibly accept cost increases, including in wages.
Thus, Australia ended up with wages growth too low to be
consistent with inflation at target, and the peculiar spectacle
of a central bank governor complaining that wages growth

was too low. And if labour remains cheap, why invest to
economise on it and thereby boost productivity?
A cost-plus mentality also amplifies the pass-through of
supply shocks. This is not quite the same thing as the ‘inflation
expectations’ seen in standard economic models of inflation,
but it is related. The stronger the tendency towards cost-plus
pricing, the worse the inflation response to a supply shock.
This is also true when inflation expectations are high, but the

distinction matters. The recent academic literature includes
some fascinating empirical evidence  from New Zealand that
suggests that beliefs about current and future overall inflation
have little bearing on many firms’ pricing decisions. As related
work using French data shows, firms’ pricing choices are
shaped less by economy-wide inflation developments than by
what is happening to their costs and in their industry.
That adds up to economy-wide behaviour that looks a lot

like firms (in aggregate) setting prices according to expected
inflation (in aggregate), but under the hood something
quite different is going on. If firms’ beliefs about future
economy-wide inflation do not matter much in their pricing
decisions, then neither does the credibility of a central bank’s
commitment to return inflation to target. The mechanism to
get inflation down then must be simply to weaken demand
enough to convince firms that cost-plus will not fly in the

market. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers
or anyone in an interest-sensitive sector when supply shocks
abound. It does, however, imply that if supply shocks fade
or even self-correct, so will high inflation. If and when that
happens, tight monetary policy can be unwound.
The deeper question is whether cost-plus price dynamics can
be reduced. That is more likely to be a question for regulators
than for central banks.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
bias towards an October or December decision. Williams
stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in
this cycle given known information. And Waller also showed
confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are

doing some of the FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest
rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating that
one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation
near the 2.0%yr medium-term target. However, as discussed
in this month’s Market Outlook , capacity pressures are likely
to see inflation consistently above target, rather than below,
in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity in the
housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to

remain modestly restrictive over the forecast horizon and will
also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade
account. At USD133.5bn, the September result was a whisker
inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More
impressively, throughout 2025 and 2026 year-to-date the
surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the
average of 2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth
of export growth is as impressive as the headline annual gain of

36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese
investment in neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time,
both trade and income receipts from offshore investment should
buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound
foreign investment should moderate the pace of gains against
other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the
evolution of producer prices across Asia, and the likelihood

of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent
decades continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was
one of the key drivers of declining goods prices. While
still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing
development to instead be driven by capital, technology and
energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking
production are also increasingly prioritising resilience, security
and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore more

probably future supply-chain developments will help contain
rising costs through productivity than drive sustained price
declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September
Labour Force Survey . Employment surprised to the
downside, –39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force
participation from August’s near-historic high largely offset.
The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August

was revised up.
Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless,
the take home point from the update is that the labour
market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs
below working-age population growth. We expect the
unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter
financial conditions and cost-of-living pressures restrict
activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast
peak is near at 4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the

communication challenge for the RBA. The September minutes
were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing
on materialising upside risks to inflation. But, with the
unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption
of 4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the
labour market is becoming harder to defend. Still, with a clear
focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our
near-term CPI forecasts in the October Market Outlook  – we

still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in
September, taking the annual rate up to 3.7%yr. Energy prices
were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation
was a touch above consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr).
Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months
(0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4% (3.1%yr) on a

0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport
services and a 0.3% increase in medical care services (2.7%yr
and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release
of the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures,
with the headline and core measures up 6.3%yr and 5.0%yr
respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these
developments, firms across multiple districts citing tariffs,
insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost

pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect
further price increases as contracts/hedges roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is
enough signal in these reports to warrant a follow-up hike
at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee
members believe they are near the end of the tightening cycle
and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman
both showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear

Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Downward revisions to 2027 outlook
This week we have updated our New Zealand economic
outlook for the next year, in particular reflecting the latest
developments in the energy market and key financial
variables (long-term interest rates and the exchange rate).

After last week’s marking down of our forecast for through-
year GDP growth in 2026 to 2.5%yr, this week we have
reduced our forecast of growth in 2027 by 0.3ppts to 2.7%yr.
This downward revision reflects a less positive assessment
of the outlooks for consumption and investment (including
residential investment) considering the more elevated path
assumed for energy prices and a higher near-term track for
long-term interest rates (as discussed in our updated Market

Outlook, published earlier this week).
A partial offset to weaker growth in domestic demand is
provided by a slightly more positive outlook for exports
(particularly exports of manufactured goods and tourism
inflows) because of a downward revision to our expectations
for the NZ dollar. We now expect NZD/USD to end this
year around current levels at 0.56 – 3c lower than expected
previously – before appreciating to around 0.64 over the
course of 2027 as interest rate differentials with the US

gradually close and as the USD appreciates. We think that the
low value of the NZ dollar – in trade-weighted terms now at a
17-year low – helps to explain the recent strong performance
in the previously moribund manufacturing sector, as well as
tourist inflows from key markets such as Australia.
Last week we raised our forecast for CPI inflation at year end
to 4.3%yr, largely on account of recent developments in global
energy prices (although the weak NZ dollar has compounded the

effect). This week we have slightly revised up our forecast for
inflation for the first half of 2027. But because energy prices are
assumed to decline in 2027 from what is now a higher starting
level, our 2027 year-end forecast for inflation has nudged
down 0.1ppts to 1.6%yr. Needless to say, forecasts of headline
inflation, in particular, are subject to significant revision given the
uncertain outlook for the Middle East conflict and uncertainty

about how that will be reflected in global energy prices.
Ahead of next week’s very important Q3 CPI report – which
could prove decisive – we have made no changes to our
forecast for the RBNZ’s policy settings. So, for now we
continue to think that despite the higher near-term inflation
outlook the RBNZ will stick with the strategy communicated
in the September Monetary Policy Statement, i.e. that at the
vv28 October meeting it will pause to assess the impact of this

year’s 50bps of policy tightening, before considering a further
upward adjustment in December.
Turning to this week’s data, the news from the housing market
remained decidedly downbeat. House sales fell a further 4% in
September – the seventh consecutive month of decline – to be
12% lower than a year earlier. With the stock of listings at an
11-year high, the median selling time remains elevated. And
so unsurprisingly, house prices fell for a fourth consecutive

month and are now down 1.6%yr. Prices remain especially
soft in the North Island, with prices down more than 3%yr in
Auckland and more than 4%yr in Wellington. Given upward
pressures on mortgage rates, broader strength in the economy
– including a stronger labour market – will likely be required to
reverse this trend. On that score, MBIE’s measure of online job
advertisements was little changed in September, continuing
the flat trend since the beginning of this year.

In other news, retail spending, as measured by electronic card
payments, decreased 0.4%m/m in September following an
unrevised 0.9% decline in August. This was a weaker result than
we had expected based on the recent sawtooth pattern and
our own internal data. We should treat these data with some
suspicion given that for quite a while now the Stats NZ series
seems not to have reflected the spending seen through banks’
own card spending networks, perhaps suggesting changes in the

payments arena may be leaving the official sector data behind.
Even so, unsurprisingly, with petrol prices rebounding late in
the month, spending on fuel increased 2.9%mth. However,
core spending, which excludes spending on fuel and vehicles,
fell 0.7%m/m. While spending on consumables (groceries)
increased modestly in September, this was at the expense of
spending on ‘nice to haves’. Spending on hospitality suffered a
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
further 2.3%mth fall following a 1.9%mth decline in August,
and spending on apparel replicated the 2.0%mth decline
reported in August. Spending on durable goods also fell
slightly in September (-0.4%mth).
Despite the weak outcome for the August and September

months, retail spending over the September quarter still
eked out a minor 0.1% gain, while core spending increased
0.4%. Given higher prices, at face value this points to a weak
quarter for volumes (next week’s CPI report will cast more
light on pricing behaviour during the quarter). It is worth
noting that total retail spending, as will be depicted in the
official quarterly retail sales survey released next month, could
point to somewhat stronger growth. Purchases of big-ticket

items that often are bought on credit – such as cars, furniture
and appliances – are underrepresented in the electronic card
spending survey.
Given subdued domestic demand, local hospitality businesses
have relied on foreign visitors to drive growth in spending.
And the good news is that visitor arrivals increased to a new
post-Covid high in August to be up 8%yr. Arrivals of Chinese
citizens have grown by almost a third to sit near pre-Covid
levels in recent months, while arrivals of US citizens grew

17%yr. However, the Middle East conflict has impacted
long-haul arrivals from Europe (UK arrivals are down 8%yr).
Meanwhile, inflows of migrants remain at modest levels,
totalling around 20,000 in the year to August – about half
what might be expected on average over the cycle.
News from the manufacturing sector remained positive in
September, with the BusinessNZ Manufacturing PMI rising
to a 3-month high of 56.5 in September. Improvement was
seen across all subcomponents of the index, with the new

orders index rising 1.8pts to 57.0. We think that the recovery in
manufacturing this year owes much to the weakness of the NZ
dollar – especially against the Australian dollar – and activity
associated with a strong domestic primary sector and an
emerging recovery in domestic residential construction activity.
Last but certainly not least, ahead of next week’s all-important
Q3 CPI, the Selected Prices report for September was firmer
than expected. That was mainly due to larger than expected

increases in airfares and holiday accommodation costs, which
offset softer than expected food prices.
Accounting for the September prices data, we’ve left our
forecast for Q3 inflation  at 0.7%. That would see the annual
inflation rate dropping back to 3.7%, down from 4.1% in the
year to June. Underlying that move has been a moderation in
fuel prices after they rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle
East war. That’s balanced against increases in council rates,

building costs and food prices.
Our forecast is lower than the RBNZ’s September MPS
forecast for a 0.8% rise (+3.9%yr). However, with some large
swings in volatile items like fuel, the RBNZ will likely focus
on measures of core inflation. Core inflation was already at
firm levels in the June quarter, with key measures sitting in
the upper part of the RBNZ’s target band. We expect that
they will take another step higher in September, to sit around

3%. Upside surprises in core inflation would be an important
development for the RBNZ ahead of the October OCR review,
as this could suggest that the lower NZD and higher oil prices
are boosting underlying price pressures by more and sooner
than they may have expected.

### Page 7

Q3 is likely to produce a very similar outcome to Q2, with 
quarterly growth of around 1.0% holding the annual rate at 
4.3%yr. Year-to-date, growth will remain around 4.7%ytd 
thanks to the Q1 result.
The detail and partial data for September will again highlight 
the weak state of the consumer and domestic-oriented 
businesses, but also the incredible strength of exports. With 
the trade surplus back at record highs, there are limits to the 
contribution possible from net exports hence, necessitating 
a strengthening of domestic demand if aggregate growth is 
to continue at the rate authorities intend into 2027. Expect 
additional stimulus through the turn of the year.

Document 238: Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-22)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September (2026-10-22).pdf

### Page 1

#businessconditions #confidence #aud #australia
Australian Business Conditions and Confidence, September
By Luka Belobrajdic
Economist, Westpac
14:15 October 13 2026 Share
Confidence posts its sharpest fall since the start of the Middle East conflict as elevated fuel
prices and higher interest rates weigh on business sentiment, while conditions hold steady
at weak levels. Conditions unchanged at -1 / Confidence -4pts to -13.

### Page 2

The September NAB Business Survey was in the field from 23 to 30 September, capturing ongoing
global uncertainty, still-rising fuel costs and, at least in part, the RBA’s September rate hike. Business
confidence continued to deteriorate, recording its sharpest fall since March and the onset of the Middle
East conflict. The index declined 4pts to -13 and, while remaining above the lows recorded earlier this
year, is now at its weakest level since May. At 18pts below its long-run average, the result underscores

the deep pessimism that continues to weigh on businesses. This broadly mirrors the pessimism evident
in the Westpac-Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index, where elevated fuel prices and higher
interest rates have coalesced in a similarly weak assessment of the outlook.
The headline business conditions index held steady in negative territory at -1, albeit rising 0.25pts on
an unrounded basis. While encouraging that conditions have not deteriorated further, the index has

now remained at -1 (rounded) for two consecutive months, its weakest level since the height of the
COVID-19 pandemic in 2020. At 7pts below its long-run average, the result points to continued
pressure across the business sector.
Across the major sub-components, employment moved into negative territory, falling 3pts to -1 and
sitting 4pts below its long-run average. This marks the first outright negative reading since November
2020 and provides further evidence of the gradual easing in labour market conditions. Against the

backdrop of compressed margins, the result may also indicate firms are becoming increasingly
cautious around hiring decisions. By contrast, trading conditions rose 1pt to 4, although they remain
8pts below their long-run average. The relative resilience in trading conditions is broadly consistent with

### Page 3

Westpac DataX Card-Tracker data, which suggests nominal spending remained resilient, albeit
slowing, through the September quarter despite ongoing headwinds facing consumers. Profitability
recorded the strongest improvement across the major components, rising 3pts to -5, although the sub-
index remains deeply subdued at 10pts below its long-run average.
Capacity utilisation edged 0.2ppt lower to 82.3%. While the monthly decline provides a tentative signal

of easing capacity pressures, utilisation remains 0.9ppt above its long-run average and continues to
trend higher on a three-month average basis. Capital expenditure continued to recover from its mid-
year weakness, rising 3pts to 7. While still below the highs recorded in the first quarter, the sub-index is
now trending higher on a three-month average basis, pointing to some stabilisation in investment
intentions.
Cost growth was comparatively subdued in September, although purchase cost pressures remain

elevated. Purchase cost growth edged 0.1ppt higher to 2.4%qtr, sitting 1.1ppt above its long-run
average and continuing to weigh on margins. By contrast, labour cost growth eased 0.1ppt to 1.7%qtr,
although it remains 0.5ppt above its long-run average. Final product price growth was unchanged at
0.8%qtr, 0.2ppt above its long-run average. The widening gap between purchase cost and final product
price growth suggests firms continue to absorb a meaningful share of higher input costs, consistent

with profitability remaining deeply below average. Looking ahead, the October survey will provide an
early indication of how the card surcharging ban is affecting business costs and the extent to which
these are passed through to final prices.

### Page 4

Related articles
Among other indicators, forward orders declined 1pt to -1, pointing to continued caution around the
demand outlook. Inventories, by contrast, rose 4pts (rounded) to 7, some 5pts above their long-run
average. In combination with weak forward orders, the inventory build points to a degree of slack
emerging across the business sector.

Document 288: Eurozone_ Improving business sentiment, driven by tech, is spreading to other sectors (2026-10-14)

14 October 2026
The bank
for a changing

EUROZONE: IMPROVING BUSINESS SENTIMENT, DRIVEN BY TECH, IS SPREADING TO OTHER SECTORS
Stéphane Colliac & Tarik Rharrab
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
The September PMI surveys confirm that growth in the Eurozone is getting stronger, boosted in particular by new export orders, which are
still rising. Sectors that were already performing well – technology and capital goods (including for defence) – are continuing to grow, while

those that had been struggling until now – aeronautics, consumer goods and financial services – are seeing their production indices recover.
This strong performance is particularly noteworthy, given that it has not yet been undermined by higher inflation.
The heatmap shows the PMI index readings: a reading above 50 indicates expansion, while a reading below 50 signals contraction compared to the previous
month. For each indicator, sectors are ranked in descending order based on their September 2026 reading.

Shades of red represent values above 50, while shades of blue represent values below 50: the intensity of the colour increases the further away they are
from this threshold.
Production growth spreads to more sectors
The PMI surveys on business sentiment confirm that business activity in the Eurozone is expanding at a faster pace and spreading to more sectors.
Household-consumption-related sectors (goods and services), which were adversely affected in the spring by the outbreak of the conflict in the Middle

East, do not appear to be negatively impacted by the rising inflation observed since August. The aeronautics sector, where production has mainly been
limited by supply constraints, is also showing signs of improvement.
SOURCE: S&P GLOBAL, BNP PARIBAS

These trends, although still moderate, come alongside a drive to upgrade equipment, fuelled by investment in digital technology, electrification
(which is supporting the automotive sector) and defence. The momentum in technology goods and services further strengthened in September.
The production index for computer and electronics hit a five-year high, while the software services production index stayed close to its July peak.

Financial services are also enjoying a significant improvement, reflecting the healthy lending momentum despite interest rates starting to rise
(see our analysis).
Growth driven by exports
Exports drove growth in the first half of 2026, both across the Eurozone as a whole (see our analysis) and among its key members (notably
Germany). This momentum is also increasing, as indicated by the ‘new export orders’ component of the various PMI indices. As was the case with

production, there has been a particularly noteworthy increase for capital goods (technology and machinery) and related services, particularly digital
services. This trend is playing out more broadly, as, in September, new export orders rose across all sub-sectors, with the exception of intermediate
goods and consumer goods, where contraction recently eased. There has been a significant improvement in the transport sector, particularly in
the aeronautics industry, which has major markets in Asia. Recent data on French exports show that aeronautics exports to this region are rising

once again.
The convergence between rising production and increasing export-order backlogs shows that Eurozone growth is based on an increasingly solid
foundation. Inflationary pressures are not holding it back for the time being.

Document 298: France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France - 2027 Budget_ Fiscal consolidation must now resume (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 1
Status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 1st October 2026

EcoFlash

2027 Budget: Fiscal consolidation must now resume

The 2027 budget, adopted by the French Council of Ministers today, calls for a reduction in France’s fiscal 
deficit to 5% of GDP in 2027 (down from 5.4% in 2026). This is a first step toward s the multi-year fiscal 
consolidation needed to stabilise the public debt-to-GDP ratio. This ratio is expected to rise further in 2027 
and until the deficit is reduced to 3% of GDP. Furthermore, given the increase in some expenditures (+0.6 pp 
of GDP for debt service and defence spending), the projected fiscal effort amounts to 1 pp. Nearly one-third 
of this effort would come from higher compulsory levies, and two -thirds from curbing government and 
social spending.

2027 Draft finance law: A step in the right direction. The government is forecasting an improvement in the fiscal 
deficit from 5.4% of GDP in 2026 to 5% in 2027. This projection is based on a credible growth assumption of 1% 
(see our analysis of French growth drivers). To achieve this target, compulsory levies (CL) are expected to rise to 
44.2% of GDP, an increase of 0.3 pp (the same increase as in 2026, compared with +0.9 pp in 2025). The public 
spending-to-GDP ratio would decline by 0.2 pp to 56.9%. The effort required to reduce the deficit to 5% is greater 
than it appears. Indeed, some expenditures will increase (interest payments by 0.4 pp and defence spending by 
0.2 pp). Consequently, the fiscal effort for next year amounts not to 0.4 pp, but to 1 pp of GDP. One-third of this will 
be achieved through increased CL, and two-thirds through spending cuts. Social spending will contribute, notably 
through the cancellation of EUR 3 billion in social contribution reductions for low-wage earners, as well as efforts 
totaling EUR 6 billion each in the areas of pensions and healthcare. Social spending shows the largest deviation 
from pre-COVID levels, with an increase representing 1.2 pp of GDP, nearly half of the gap between the 2026 
budget balance and a 3% deficit. Government spending, excluding interest payments and defence, is expected to 
be frozen in nominal terms.

A compromise seems likely despite a tight schedule. The French National Assembly will start reviewing the 
budget on Tuesday 13 October. The government will need to ensure that a majority vote against the draft budget 
does not form. The current circumstances, including increasing sovereign bond yields and the upcoming 
presidential election, call for a consensus based on fiscal consolidation of the size proposed by the government. If 
Parliament does not pass a budget by the end of the year, the government will have to choose between 
implementing the government’s budget proposal by executive order ( only possible if there was no parliamentary 
vote) or through a special law (as in 2025 and 2026). A special law would enable tax collection , while the 
government and social security agencies could continue to issue debt. In this case, the 2026 budget would apply 
in 2027 (without indexation) until Parliament passes a budget in the first few weeks of the year. This must occur 
before the end of February (when Parliament’s session ends, earlier than usual due to the presidential election).

The one-year postponement of the 5% budget deficit target has a measurable impact. The gap between the 
budget adopted for 2026 (deficit of 5% of GDP) and the actual budget outcome (deficit of 5.4%) is due to 
unforeseeable circumstances (the conflict in Iran  and extreme drought). However, postponing the deficit target  
previously set for 2026 to 2027 comes at a cost: 1) the public debt-to-GDP ratio is expected to reach 121% in 2027 
according to our forecasts (1 pp above our scenario with a 5% target in 2026 and a deficit reduced to 4.5% in 2027), 
and 2) the (upcoming) postponement to 2032 of the 3% of GDP deficit target (a commitment that most presidential 
candidates plan to make) rather than in 2029 (the government had committed to this with the European Commission 
in 2024). In 2032, the public debt-to-GDP ratio would stabilise at 124% of GDP (instead of 121.5% if the deficit had 
been reduced to 5% of GDP as early as 2026 and to 3% of GDP by the end of the decade).

Another delay in fiscal consolidation would further erode France’s credibility. This new delay could stem 
either from a decision by the government formed in May 2027 to immediately implement part of its agenda, or from 
the enactment of a special law that would remain in effect until (just after) the likely legislative elections in June  
2027  or  for  the  entire  year.  If  the  government  emerging  from  the  elections  were  to  resume  fiscal  
consolidation only in 2028 (with a deficit of 5% of GDP that it projects to reduce to 3% by 2032), public debt would 
reach 126% of GDP by that date (a one-year delay in fiscal consolidation would result in an additional 2 pp of 
debt). This further delay could potentially undermine the credibility of the next government, given that budget 
targets were  not met in three out of four years between 2023 and 2026, a situation that would be costly amid a 
sharp rise in long-term interest rates. Furthermore, a higher deficit would increase the amount of debt to be issued 
within an already  tight schedule. The Agence France Trésor (AFT) has announced debt issuances totaling EUR 
340 billion in 2027 (an  increase  of  EUR  20  billion  compared  to  2026).  This  increase  is  due  to  the  maturing  
COVID  related  debt (beginning in 2027 and continuing largely through 2030), while the amount of debt to be 
issued to finance the deficit will remain stable compared to 2026. A decrease from this last driver would be needed 
in subsequent years so that  the AFT’s debt issuance can stabilise.

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of the Advanced Economies Team, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

Seven of the ten sectors in the index expect to see an improvement in revenues relative to Q3 2025. The Energy 
sector has the highest revenue growth rate for the quarter, while the Real Estate has the weakest anticipated growth 
compared to Q3 2025.

The Energy sector has the highest revenue growth rate (53.7%) of any sector. It is expected to generate sales of 
€333.3B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €216.9B in Q3 2025.

The Real Estate sector has the lowest revenue growth rate (-15.3%) of any sector. It is expected to generate sales of 
€1.8B in Q3 2026, compared to sales of €2.2B in Q3 2025.

Document 385: Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed

Source PDF: all report pdf/Top of Mind - Assessing a Less Transparent Fed.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs (Top of Mind)
Date: 2026-08-25
Pages: 26
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Goldman Sachs Group, Inc.
Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less transparent era. So, is that
a positive or negative development, and what could it mean for markets and the
economy? We speak with Former Fed governors Donald Kohn and Stephen Miran
as well as GS’ Jan Hatzius who agree that rigid “Odyssean” forward guidance that
commits policymakers to a pre-set path risks policy mistakes and is therefore rarely
appropriate. But they somewhat disagree about the benefits of great er transparency

around the Fed’s reaction function. Hatzius and Kohn argue that such transparency
helps markets better anticipate and price in policy changes, accelerating monetary
policy transmission. But Miran believes that the line between committing to a path
and being transparent is clearer in theory than in practice. And while GS economists
and strategists find that reduc ed transparency leads to increased market volatility,
the question is whether this just increases the market’s noise or also improves the

market’s signal. Hatzius argues largely for the former and Miran for the latter.
Jan Hatzius, Chief Economist and Head of Global Investment Research, Goldman Sachs
“Markets price what they think the Fed will do, not what they think the Fed should do. That will remain true even if the Fed
obscures its reaction function… market guesses about the Fed’s next moves will simply become worse, leading to more
volatility... and importantly, volatility that serves no constructive economic purpose.”

Donald Kohn, former Vice Chairman of the Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System, Robert V . Roosa
Chair in International Economics and Senior Fellow, Brookings Institution
“I sympathize with Chairman Warsh to some extent. [But] the real problem with Warsh’s approach is his reluctance to articulate
how the FOMC sees the economy evolving… explaining the Fed’s thinking about the economic outlook is essential in helping
markets act as a stabilizing force and is a core component of accountability.”

ISSUE 151 | August 19, 2026 | 1:30 PM EDT

### Page 3

El
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 3 
Top of Mind Issue 151

New Fed Chairman Kevin Warsh is steering the Fed into a less 
transparent era characterized by shorter post-meeting 
statements, less forward guidance, and a more limited role for 
projections, marking a meaningful break from the 
transparency revolution that has defined central banking over 
the last quarter century. So, what could a less transparent Fed 
mean for markets and the economy? 
For answers, we turn to former Fed governors Donald Kohn, 
now Robert V. Roosa Chair in International Economics and 
senior fellow at the Brookings Institution, and Stephen Miran, 
now senior strategist at Hudson Bay Capital Management, as 
well as Jan Hatzius, GS’ Chief Economist and Head of Global 
Investment Research. 
We first ask whether scaling back Fed communication is a 
positive or negative development. Hatzius says it’s likely 
negative, though he draws a clear line between transparency 
and forward guidance. He argues that clarity about the Fed’s 
reaction function helps markets better anticipate policy 
changes and quickly incorporate new information into financial 
conditions, thereby accelerating monetary policy transmission 
to the economy. So, he believes that unwinding that 
transparency would be a mistake. (Note: GS Global 
Economists Joseph Briggs and Megan Peters lay out the 
evidence backing this argument on pgs. 14-15.) 
But Hatzius takes a more nuanced view of forward guidance, 
arguing that rigid “Odyssean” guidance that commits 
policymakers to a pre-set path regardless of new information 
only makes sense at the effective lower bound for the policy 
rate, while conditional “Delphic” guidance tied to the 
economic outlook is generally more defensible. Although 
markets could confuse the two, he believes that most 
sophisticated investors understand the difference and is 
therefore not particularly worried that markets have become 
overly reliant on the policy dots published in the Fed’s 
Summary of Economic Projections (SEP). Instead, he 
considers them a “useful source of additional information 
about the Fed’s reaction function” (except for the long-run 
policy dot). If anything, Hatzius would prefer the dots move 
in an even more informative direction—by publishing each 
policymaker’s combined projections for growth, inflation, 
unemployment, and the policy rate on an anonymized basis. 
Kohn agrees that clarity about the Fed’s “narrative” and 
reaction function is vital. Explaining how the Fed sees the 
economy evolving and how policy would respond, he says, is 
“essential in helping markets act as a stabilizing force and is 
a core component of accountability.” Still, he sympathizes 
somewhat with Chairman Warsh’s view that the dot plot is 
“not helpful” (see pg. 9 for Warsh’s own words on this), 
arguing that the market’s hyper-focus on the SEP’s median 
policy dot “illustrates the risk of excessive precision and 
false certainty,” as small shifts in it can trigger unwarranted 
market moves. So, he has long favored omitting the median. 
Miran, though, is more supportive of Warsh’s push for a less 
transparent Fed. Like Hatzius and Kohn, he’s concerned that 
excessive forward guidance that locks the Fed into a policy 
path raises the risk of policy errors and also argues that such 
guidance can impair market pricing of risk. But he’s less 
convinced about the benefits of increased transparency 
around the Fed’s reaction function, making the case that the 
distinction between the Fed committing to a pre-set policy 
path and being transparent about its reaction function is clear 
in theory, but less so in practice. So, he thinks “the policy dots 
absolutely have to go” but feels less strongly about omitting 
the SEP’s economic projections. 
But would less transparency allow markets to provide the Fed 
with more “direct and unfiltered” information about the 
economy, as Warsh has argued? The answer seems to boil 
down to the implications for market volatility, and whether any 
resulting volatility would simply increase the market’s noise or 
also improve its signal. 
As for the volatility implications, the evidence is fairly clear. 
Briggs and Peters find that increased central bank 
transparency has led to a reduction in market volatility over the 
last several decades. This is consistent with GS Head of US 
Rates Strategy William Marshall’s recent observation of the 
reverse: that the US rates market has already priced 
substantial uncertainty associated with a less transparent Fed, 
with the July FOMC representing the largest non-cut meeting 
day surprise in almost three decades.  
But Marshall argues that whether such meeting-day surprises 
lead to structurally higher rates volatility depends (again) on 
whether the Fed’s reaction function is well understood. If it’s 
not, he would expect higher rates volatility to persist. GS FX 
Strategists Michael Cahill and Lexi Kanter find a similar 
pattern in FX: that central bank communication shifts can 
change which events and data drive FX volatility without 
necessarily raising it, but less transparency could increase 
Dollar volatility—all else equal (though global macro 
conditions remain its biggest driver).  
But the key debate seems to be whether the increased 
volatility resulting from less Fed transparency will improve or 
worsen the market’s signal. Hatzius is clearly in the “worsen” 
camp. He argues that markets price what they think the Fed 
will do—not what it should do—and less transparency won’t 
change that; it would simply produce worse guesses. So, he 
says that while volatility in and of itself is not necessarily bad, 
volatility driven by uncertainty about the Fed’s reaction 
function would serve no constructive economic purpose. 
Kohn agrees that the lack of a clear Fed narrative could create 
damaging uncertainty. But he argues that removing overly 
specific guidance could help free markets to process new 
information more independently and isn’t too worried about 
modestly higher volatility as a result. And Miran seems most 
convinced that reducing transparency would improve the 
markets’ signal, with volatility an unavoidable but acceptable 
cost: “The first best world of signal without volatility doesn't 
exist. I'd rather have that signal, even if it's messy, than 
throw the signal out by anchoring market expectations to the 
Fed's near-term projections.”
